Let's analyze Kronecker product mathematically. Let A length n, B length m. C_{im+j}=A_i B_j. D=C^2 coefficient K:
D_K = ∑_{s} A*A_s * B*B_{K - s m}, where K - s m in [0,2m-2]. Thus for each K, at most two s (except maybe). Let U=floor(K/m), v=K-Um. D_K = E_U * F_v + E_{U-1} * F_{v+m} if valid (where E=convA length 2n-1, F=convB length 2m-1, F_{v+m} for overflow). Max D ≤ max_i E_i * (F_v + F_{v+m} for some) or E_{i}F_v+E_{i-1}F_{v+m}. If E and F nonnegative. If there is a block index where E is max and F_v+F_{v+m} small maybe lower. But max could be roughly maxE * max(F) * (if E_{i} and E_{i-1} both high). If A maximally at multiple adjacent blocks, can be 2 maxE maxF. If maxE isolated, maybe factor less. Uniform A all E_i high adjacent, factor maybe? For all ones, E triangular; max at center block, adjacent similar, F max=2? But D max =nm. Product maxE maxF = n*m*2? Wait B all ones length m: F max=m, E max=n, product 2? For K center K=(n-1)m+(m-1)? D=nm, E_U? Let's numeric: all ones c length nm, D max=nm. E max=n, F max=m; product=nm. no factor2? There is only one E contribution at exact center? Depends. Detailed LP update:
Given f (sum normalized to some T, e.g. T=1 for simplicity). Compute M = max conv(f,f). We wish find g≥0 such that conv(f,g)≤M (or maybe ≤M*slack) and maximize sum g. Let opt_sum = max ∑g. Rescale g to sum T: g_res = g * T/opt_sum. Since conv(f,g_res) = (T/opt_sum) conv(f,g) ≤ (T/opt_sum) M. If opt_sum ≥ T, then scale down. Update f←(1-t)f+t g_res. Why would max conv(f,f) reduce? If g_res is a "dual" direction, maybe. The old description: Starting nonnegative f normalized sum√(2n), M set. Compute g by maximizing sum subject to ||f*g||∞≤M. Rescale and update; repetition produces nonincreasing ||f*f||∞.

We can test with all ones length n. f sum T, M≈T^2/n. LP maximize sum g s.t. f*g≤M. If f uniform, constraints conv(1,g)≤M. The maximum sum g under all sliding window sums ≤M and g≥0. For n large, this is like bounded moving sums; max sum ~ n M? If M =T^2/n, sum max maybe ~? Let's compute: constraints for k near center: sum all g≤M? Actually conv uniform: for k=0 g0≤M; k=n-1 sum all g≤M. Since full length n, central constraint sums all g ≤M. Thus opt_sum≤M. But f sum T; M=T^2/n << T for n large, so opt_sum <T. Better: Use linearized active LP as derivative-free improvement. Starting from known good small sequences, it can refine.

Let's design a generic local optimizer LocalSearch:
- Input a normalized sum=1, length n.
- Iterate:
   1. Compute c, M, score.
   2. Active indices: c >= M - active_tol*M (e.g., 1e-4) and maybe top K.
   3. Build linearized direction LP:
       Variables h (n) and δ. Objective max δ. Constraints:
          For each active k: ∑_r d_{k,r} h_r + δ ≤ 0, where d_{k,r}=2 a_{k-r} if valid.
          ∑h=0.
          -a_i - eps ≤ h_i ≤ U (e.g., 5*max(a,1/n) or 1 maybe).
       This is LP with 2n+1 variables, len(A)+1 constraints. Could be small for n≤200. But building dense each iter. Use scipy.linprog. The optimum δ maybe zero for symmetric/stalled.
       We can also solve direction to minimize max linearized (same).
       If δ>tol, propose a1 = normalize(a + h) (sum already 1). Do line search: for t in [1,0.5,0.25,0.1, ...], b=normalize(a+t*h); if score improves accept. Else reject.
   4. If no LP improvement, add random perturbation (block perturb) to escape, then repeat.
   5. Optionally run LP SoA update.
- Use time limit, keep best.

Let's implement objective/conv exactly:
```
def conv_fast(a):
    n=a.size
    if n<=4096 maybe direct np.convolve? np.convolve for 4000 O16M okay but many eval; FFT faster for >512.
    L=1
    while L < 2*n-1: L <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L); c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L); return c[:2*n-1]
```
FFT result tiny negative; clip max with max. Direct for n≤256 to avoid FFT error.
`score_array(a)`:
```
a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
s=a.sum()
if s<0.01: return np.inf
n=a.size
c=conv_fast(a)
M=c.max()
return float(2*n*M/(s*s))
```

```
   # Use linprog maximize sum g: c_obj=-ones

   # get g, sum_g. if sum_g > T*0.5: g = g*T/sum_g; return mix
```

```
rows=[]; cols=[]; data=[]
for i,av in enumerate(a):
  if av>1e-15:
    # a_i contributes to coefficients k = i + j, j=0..n-1 -> k range i..i+n-1
    ks = np.arange(i, i+n)
    rows.extend(ks); cols.extend(np.full(n,i)); data.extend(av*np.ones(n))
```

Blockperturb:
```
def perturb(a,rng,mode):
   b=a.copy()
   n=len(b)
   if mode==0: block width w int(rng.integers(max(1,n//20), max(2,n//4))); start; b[start:stop]*=exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,stop-start)); 
   if mode==1: move mass: choose two blocks, subtract from one and add to other, clip.
   if mode==2: set block to previous best block + noise.
   normalize.
```

Alternative use active subgradient with few active coefficients: For active indices K small, g_i = average over k of a_{k-i}. So multiplicative update:
```
active = indices where c >= M - rel_tol*M (top max K)
g = zeros
for k in active:
   seg = a[max(0,k-n+1):min(n,k+1)]  # a_{k-i} i range? if i from lo to hi, k-i from hi? Let's test: i=lo, j=k-lo; i=hi, j=k-hi. We need a_j. The segment of a indices from k-hi to k-lo. We can do:
   lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
   # j range k-hi .. k-lo
   jlo=max(0,k-hi); jhi=min(n-1,k-lo)
   g[lo:hi+1] += a[jlo:jhi+1]
g /= len(active)
a *= exp(-eta * 2*g); normalize.
```
Simplify: for each i, j=k-i valid if 0<=j<n. The slice length = hi-lo+1. `seg = a[k-hi : k-lo+1]` because jlo=k-hi, jhi=k-lo. Yes.

```
def build_conv_matrix(n, a):
   rows=[]; cols=[]; data=[]
   # For each variable g_j, it appears in constraints k=j+i for i=0..n-1 with coeff a_i
   for j in range(n):
      if a[j]!=0? data a[0:n]
      # constraints k=j..j+n-1
      rows.extend(range(j, j+n))
      cols.extend([j]*n)
      data.extend(a) # length n
   A=csr_matrix((data,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n))
```

Let's implement `fast_local` robustly:
```
def fast_local(a, end_time, rng, max_iters=10000):
    a=np.clip(np.asarray(a,float),0,1000)
    s=a.sum(); if s<=1e-12: return None, inf
    a=a/s
    best=a.copy(); bs=score_norm(a)
    improves=0
    i=0
    while time.monotonic() < end_time and improves < 500:
       # subgrad steps
       for eta in etas:
          b, sc = subgrad_candidate(a,best? )
       # perturb blocks
       for _ in range(5):
          b = random_perturb(a,rng)
          if norm_score(b) < best_score: update
       if no improvement in 20 iterations: add stronger perturbation to a? or break
    return best,bs
```

```
def generate_inits(n, rng, best_small=None):
    cands=[]
    # baseline [2,1] padded? For any n, sequence with first r [2,1] and rest? If append zeros bad. But we can distribute shape scaled by powers?
    # For n=2 return [2,1]; n=3 maybe [2,1,1]? include.
    if n==2: cands.append(np.array([2.0,1.0]))
    if n==4: cands.append(np.array([1,1,0,1]))
    # random uniforms
    for _ in range(3): cands.append(rng.random(n)+1e-6)
    # binary densities
    for p in [0.2,0.5,0.8]: cands.append((rng.random(n)<p).astype(float))
    # smooth Gaussian centered with sigma 0.1..0.6
    x=np.arange(n)-0.5*(n-1)
    for sig in [0.15,0.3,0.5,0.75]: cands.append(np.exp(-x*x/(2*sig*sig)))
    # endpoint taper windows
    t=x/(0.5*(n-1)+1e-12)
    for alpha in [0.5,1,2,4]: cands.append((1 - np.clip(np.abs(t),0,1)**alpha)) maybe nonnegative; zero endpoints.
    # alternating high/low
    for ratio in [0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0]:
        alt=np.ones(n); alt[1::2]=ratio; cands.append(alt)
    # cosine offsets
    for f in [1,2,3]:
      for amp in [0.5,0.9]:
        base=1+amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*f*np.arange(n)/n + rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
        cands.append(np.clip(base,1e-6,None))
    if best_small is not None and len(best_small)>=2 and n>len(best_small):
       x0=np.linspace(0,1,len(best_small)); x1=np.linspace(0,1,n)
       cands.append(np.interp(x1,x0,best_small))
    # best_small repeated with random phase? Maybe.
    return cands
```
Normalize later.

Detailed helper implementations:

```
def _clip_normalize(a, target=1.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim!=1 or a.size==0: return np.array([1.0])
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a=np.ones(a.size); s=a.size
    a=a*(target/s)
    return a
```
Score:
```
def _convolution(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    if n<=300: # direct? np.convolve maybe C fast for up to 1000? Use 1000.
       c=np.convolve(a,a,mode='full')
       c=np.maximum(c,0.0) # small neg?
       return c
    size=1
    need=2*n-1
    while size<need: size<<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,size)
    c=c[:need]
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    return c
```
Direct for n=3000 maybe okay but many eval; use FFT for >512. Threshold 300 good.

`score_norm(a)`: assumes sum approx 1? For speed during local, can compute normalized score without sum:
```
n=a.size; if n==0 return inf
s=a.sum()
if s<0.01: return inf
c=_convolution(a); M=c.max()
return float(2*n*M/(s*s))
```
This works for any scale. We can use `score_norm` everywhere.

`exact_score_list(lst)`: use np.convolve direct for reliable (maybe if list length <=5000). Else use score_norm. For final official maybe evaluate_sequence.

Subgradient candidate:
```
def subgradient_proposals(a, K=16, rel=1e-5, etas=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8,1.5)):
    n=a.size
    c=_convolution(a)
    M=c.max()
    if M<=0: return []
    # active top K
    if K > c.size: K=c.size
    # find top indices without sorting all? c.argsort(-K) small O(n log n); n up to 5000 okay but many. Could use threshold.
    # Use argsort for simplicity but repeated heavy. For n large, argsort 2n log 2n ~ moderate. Could optimize: if n>2000, use threshold.
    active=np.argsort(c)[::-1][:K]
    g=np.zeros(n)
    count=0
    for k in active:
        lo=max(0,k-n+1)
        hi=min(n-1,k)
        jlo=k-hi
        jhi=k-lo
        if jhi>=jlo:
            g[lo:hi+1] += a[jlo:jhi+1]
        count+=1
    if count==0: return []
    g /= count
    gm=g.max()
    if gm<=0: return []
    out=[]
    # multiplicative
    for eta in etas:
        b=a*np.exp(-eta*(g/gm))
        s=b.sum()
        if s>1e-15:
           b=(b/s)*a.sum() if a.sum()>0 else b/s
           out.append((b,score_norm(b)))
    # additive
    for eta in etas:
        b=a - eta*(g/gm)
        s=np.abs(b).max()
        if s>0:
           b=np.clip(b,0,None)
           bs=b.sum()
           if bs>1e-15:
              b=b*a.sum()/bs
              out.append((b,score_norm(b)))
    return out
```
Note: Multiplicative normalization preserves target sum. Good.

But computing c each subgradient call and score for each candidate (conv again) expensive. We can modify to compute score of b separately. For small n okay; for large, many candidate conv heavy. Limit etas and candidates.

Random perturb:
```
def random_perturbation(a, rng):
    n=a.size; b=a.copy(); mode=rng.integers(0,5)
    if mode==0:
      w=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//50), max(2,n//4+1)))
      st=int(rng.integers(0,n-w+1))
      sigma=float(rng.choice([0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8]))
      b[st:st+w]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,w))
    elif mode==1:
      w1=...; w2=...
      st1... st2...
      f=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.8))
      mass=b[st1:st1+w1].sum()
      if mass>0:
         b[st1:st1+w1]*=(1-f)
         add=f*mass/max(1,w2)
         b[st2:st2+w2]+=add
    elif mode==2:
      w=...; st=...
      mean=b[st:st+w].mean()
      scale=max(mean, b.max()*0.1, 1e-6)
      b[st:st+w]=rng.exponential(scale, w)
    elif mode==3:
      b=0.5*(a + a[::-1])
    else:
      idx=int(rng.integers(0,n))
      mass=a[idx]
      if mass>0:
        b[idx]=0
        w=...; st=...
        b[st:st+w]+=mass/w
    return _clip_normalize(b, a.sum() if a.size else 1.0)
```
Need ensure w not >n. Use helper `_rand_block(rng,n,minf,maxf)` top-level.

`fast_local(a,end_time,rng,allow_lp=False)`:
```
def fast_local(a,end_time,rng):
    a=_clip_normalize(a,1.0)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score_norm(a); count=0
    no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic()<end_time:
       improved=False
       # subgradient props
       props=subgradient_proposals(best,K=12,etas=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5))
       for b,sc in props:
          if sc<best_score:
             best=b; best_score=sc; improved=True; break
       if not improved:
          for _ in range(3):
             b=random_perturbation(best,rng)
             sc=score_norm(b)
             if sc<best_score:
                best=b; best_score=sc; improved=True; break
       if improved: no_improve=0; count+=1
       else:
          no_improve+=1
          if no_improve>30:
             # bigger random perturbation
             # do 10 perturb, accept best
             for _ in range(10): ...
             if no_improve>100: break
       if count>500: break
    return best,best_score
```
For n large, subgradient with K=12 and score a few convs. Could be okay. But if no improvement and random, many conv. For n=1000, each conv FFT ~0.3ms? Python overhead maybe 1ms; 1000 eval/s? okay. 2400s can many. But LP heavier.

SoA optimize:
```
def soa_optimize(a,max_time,rng):
   a=_clip_normalize(a,1.0)
   T=math.sqrt(max(1,a.size))
   aT=a*T
   best=aT.copy(); best_score=score_norm(aT)
   steps=0; fails=0
   while time.monotonic()<max_time and steps<200:
      M=_convolution(best).max()
      if M<=0: break
      n=best.size
      if n<=150:
          rows=np.arange(2*n-1)
      else:
          c=_convolution(best)
          # we already have c; avoid recompute? Use c for M; rows top min(120,2n)
          top= int(max(20,min(120,2*n-1)))
          rows=np.argsort(c)[-top:][::-1]
      q=float(rng.choice([1.0,1.0,1.2,1.5,2.0]))
      U=20*T
      x=soa_lp(best[T? Wait best already T], M*q, rows, n, U, time_limit=3.0)
      steps+=1
      accepted=False
      if x is not None:
         sum_g=x.sum()
         if sum_g>1e-12:
            g=x*(T/sum_g)
            for mix in [0.2,0.4,0.7,1.0]:
                b=(1-mix)*best + mix*g
                b=_clip_normalize(b,T)
                sc=score_norm(b)
                if sc<best_score:
                    best=b; best_score=sc; accepted=True; break
      if not accepted:
         fails+=1
         # random perturb best T
         if fails>5:
             b=random_perturbation(best,rng)
             b=_clip_normalize(b,T)
             sc=score_norm(b)
             if sc<best_score:
                 best=b; best_score=sc; fails=0
             else:
                 fails+=1
         else: fails=1
      else: fails=0
   return best/best.size? Wait best sum T; return _clip_normalize(best,1.0)
```
Need `soa_lp(aT, b_ub, rows, n, U, time_limit)` top-level.
```
def soa_lp(a,b_ub,rows,n,U,time_limit):
    if a.size==0: return None
    rows=np.asarray(rows,dtype=int)
    rows=rows[(rows>=0)&(rows<=2*n-2)]
    if rows.size==0: return None
    # build sparse for rows
    row_idx=[]; col_idx=[]; data=[]
    for k in rows:
       lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
       # variable j=lo..hi? Actually j ranges? For variable g_j, coefficient in row k is a_{k-j} if 0≤k-j<n. j from max(0,k-n+1) to min(n-1,k).
       for j in range(lo,hi+1):
           i=k-j
           val=a[i]
           if val>0:
              row_idx.append(k)
              col_idx.append(j)
              data.append(val)
    if not data: return dense? If all zero row, constraint 0≤b_ub always; omit.
    A=csr_matrix((data,(row_idx,col_idx)), shape=(len(rows),n))
    c_obj=-np.ones(n)
    bounds=[(0.0,U)]*n
    try:
       sol=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(len(rows), b_ub), bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve':True, 'time_limit':max(0.1,time_limit)})
       if sol.success and sol.x is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(sol.x)):
          return np.clip(sol.x,0,U)
    except Exception:
       pass
    return None
```
Building `A` with Python loops over each row and each j: rows top 120, per row up to n -> for n=1000 120k loop in Python slow. Use vectorized per row:

```
vals=a[js?] for variable j, coeff a[k-j]; i = k - js, reversed? If js increasing, i decreasing. vals = a[k-js] where k-js array. Use slicing: i_lo=k-hi, i_hi=k-lo. vals = a[i_lo:i_hi+1][::-1]? js lo..hi => i k-lo down to k-hi. So vals = a[k-lo : k-hi-1 : -1] if? Simpler: vals = np.array([a[k-j] for j in js]) (loop) maybe okay for 120k? Could be slow. Use `vals = a[k-hi : k-lo+1][::-1]`?    row_idx.extend(np.full(hi-lo+1,k))
   col_idx.extend(js)
   data.extend(vals)
```

```
def _rand_block(rng,n,max_fraction=0.25,min_fraction=0.001):
    minw=max(1,int(n*min_fraction))
    maxw=max(minw+1,int(n*max_fraction))
    if maxw>n: maxw=n
    w=int(rng.integers(minw,maxw+1)) if maxw>minw else minw
    st=int(rng.integers(0,n-w+1))
    return st,w
```
But if minw==maxw, rng.integers(low, high) high exclusive? Use if.

```
def exact_score_list(lst):
   arr=np.asarray(lst,float)
   arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
   s=arr.sum()
   if s<0.01: return np.inf
   n=arr.size
   c=np.convolve(arr,arr,mode='full') if n<=8000 else _convolution(arr)
   M=np.max(c)
   return float(2*n*M/(s*s))
```

Let's verify subgradient slice mapping with small example. a=[a0,a1,a2], k=1 active. lo=max(0,1-3+1= -1)=0, hi=min(2,1)=1. jlo=k-hi=0, jhi=k-lo=1. slice a[0:3]? jlo:jhi+1 = a[0:3]? Wait j values i? For i=0..1, j=1-i =>1,0. We need add a_j to g_i. For i=0 (g0), j=1 ->a1; i=1, j=0 ->a0. `generate_inits`:
```
def generate_inits(n, rng, best_small):
    out=[]
    n=int(n)
    if n<2: return []
    if n==2: out.append(np.array([2.0,1.0]))
    if n==4: out.append(np.array([1.0,1.0,0.0,1.0]))
    for _ in range(2):
       out.append(rng.random(n)+1e-8)
    for p in (0.25,0.5,0.75):
       out.append((rng.random(n)<p).astype(float)+1e-8)
    x=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
    c=n*0.5
    for sig in (0.15,0.3,0.5,0.8):
       out.append(np.exp(-((x-c)/(max(1.0,sig*max(1.0,n-1))))**2))
    t=x/(n-1) if n>1 else np.zeros(n)
    for alpha in (0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0):
       w=1.0-np.clip(np.abs(t-0.5)*2,0,1)**alpha
       out.append(w+1e-8)
    for ratio in (0.3,0.5,0.7):
       alt=np.ones(n); alt[1::2]=ratio; out.append(alt)
    for f in (1,2,3):
       phi=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
       out.append(1.0+0.9*np.cos(2*np.pi*f*x/n + phi))
    if best_small is not None:
       m=len(best_small)
       if m>=2:
          if m==n:
             out.append(np.asarray(best_small,float).copy())
          else:
             out.append(np.interp(np.linspace(0,1,n), np.linspace(0,1,m), np.asarray(best_small,float)))
    for out in out? ensure nonneg: np.clip(a,1e-12,None) maybe.
    return out
```

Let's write `subgradient_candidates`:
```
def subgradient_candidates(a,K,etas):
    n=a.size; c=_convolution(a); M=c.max()
    if M<=0: return []
    K=int(min(K,c.size))
    if K==0: return []
    active=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
    g=np.zeros(n)
    count=0
    for k in active:
       ...
       g[lo:hi+1] += seg
       count+=1
    if count==0 or g.max()<=0: return []
    g/=count
    gm=g.max()
    target=a.sum()
    out=[]
    # multiplicative
    for eta in etas:
       b=a*np.exp(-eta*(g/gm))
       s=b.sum(); if s>1e-15: out.append(b*target/s)
    # additive maybe only if n<=200
    if n<=200:
       for eta in etas:
          b=a - eta*(g/gm)
          s=np.abs(b).max()
          if s>0:
             b=np.clip(b,0.0,None); bs=b.sum()
             if bs>1e-15: out.append(b*target/bs)
    return out
```
Segment slice length must match hi-lo+1. `a[k-hi : k-lo+1][::-1]` length? If hi-lo+1. Example lo=2,hi=3,k=5,n=4: k-hi=2,k-lo+1=4 -> a[2:4] length2 reversed [a3,a2]. Good. If lo=hi=3,k=5: k-hi=2,k-lo+1=3 -> a[2:3] length1, g[3:4] += a[2]. correct.

```
def _random_span(rng,n):
    minw=max(1,int(n*0.005))
    maxw=max(minw+1,int(n*0.2))
    if maxw>n: maxw=n
    if maxw>minw:
       w=int(rng.integers(minw,maxw+1))
    else: w=minw
    st=int(rng.integers(0,n-w+1)) if n>w else 0
    return st,w
```
Use for both source/target; if source and target overlap, okay maybe.

```
def fast_local(a,end_time,rng):
    a=_clip_normalize(a,1.0)
    current=a.copy(); current_score=score_norm(current)
    gb=current.copy(); gb_score=current_score
    temp=0.03; stall=0; it=0; max_it = 20000 if a.size<50 else 2000
    while time.monotonic()<end_time and it<max_it:
       it+=1
       cand_list=[]
       etas=(0.1,0.2,0.5) if a.size<500 else (0.2,0.5)
       K=12 if a.size<200 else 6
       cand_list.extend(subgradient_candidates(current,K,etas))
       cand_list.append(random_perturbation(current,rng))
       if a.size<200: cand_list.append(random_perturbation(current,rng))
       accepted_current=False
       global_improved=False
       for cand in cand_list:
           sc=score_norm(cand)
           if np.isfinite(sc):
              if sc < gb_score:
                  gb=np.asarray(cand,float).copy(); gb_score=float(sc); global_improved=True
              if sc < current_score or (temp>1e-12 and np.log(max(rng.random(),1e-15)) < (current_score-sc)/temp):
                  current=np.asarray(cand,float).copy(); current_score=float(sc); accepted_current=True
                  if global_improved: current=gb.copy(); current_score=gb_score
                  break
       if not accepted_current:
           # extra random if stall
           if stall < 8:
              for _ in range(4):
                 cand=random_perturbation(current,rng); sc=score_norm(cand)
                 if np.isfinite(sc) and (sc < current_score or (temp>1e-12 and np.log(max(rng.random(),1e-15)) < (current_score-sc)/temp)):
                    current=cand; current_score=sc; accepted_current=True
                    break
           else:
              # reset to global best and cool
              current=gb.copy(); current_score=gb_score; temp=0.02; stall=0
       if accepted_current: stall=0
       else:
           stall+=1
           if stall % 20 == 0: temp=max(0.001,temp*0.8)
           if stall > 300: break
       # If current is much worse than global, reset
       if current_score > gb_score * 1.05 or not np.isfinite(current_score):
           current=gb.copy(); current_score=gb_score
    return gb, gb_score
```

```python
"""
Search for low-scoring non-negative step functions.

The evaluator computes
    score = 2 * n * max(np.convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a)^2).

The program combines:
  - cheap multiplicative subgradient steps on the active convolution lags,
  - random block / mass movements,
  - a linear-programming "support" update inspired by the previous SOTA
    (maximize sum(g) under a*g <= current_max),
  - multi-length multi-start search,
  - final exact/official validation and safe fallbacks.

All helpers are top-level and no closures/lambdas are used.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog, differential_evolution
    from scipy.sparse import csr_matrix
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False


def _clip_normalize(a, target=1.0):
    """Clip to [0,1000], remove non-finite values, and normalize to target sum."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1 or a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(int(max(1, t_area if False else 1)), dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(a.size)
    a = a * (float(target) / s)
    return a


def _convolution(a):
    """Full self-convolution. Direct for small n, FFT for bigger n."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 300:
        c = np.convolve(a, a, mode="full")
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        return c
    need = 2 * n - 1
    size = 1
    while size < need:
        size <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, size)[:need]
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def _score_array(a):
    """Fast score. Uses identical algebra to evaluate_sequence, without list conversion."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return np.inf
    c = _convolution(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def _exact_score_list(seq):
    """Exact direct convolution score for final validation, with safe fallback."""
    a = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return np.inf
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    if n <= 8000:
        c = np.convolve(a, a, mode="full")
    else:
        c = _convolution(a)
    m = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def _subgradient_candidates(a, k_active, etas):
    """
    Build several subgradient candidates from the active convolution lags.

    For c_k = sum_i a_i a_{k-i}, derivative with respect to a_i is
    2 * a_{k-i} when valid.  We average over the current top lags and make
    small multiplicative/additive steps in the negative subgradient.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return []
    target = float(a.sum())
    c = _convolution(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return []
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if m <= 0 or not np.isfinite(m):
        return []

    k_active = int(max(1, min(k_active, c.size)))
    if k_active < c.size:
        active = np.argpartition(c, -k_active)[-k_active:]
    else:
        active = np.arange(c.size)

    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    count = 0
    for k in active:
        k = int(k)
        lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        if hi < lo:
            continue
        # For i = lo..hi, j = k-i runs from k-lo down to k-hi.
        seg = a[k - hi:k - lo + 1][::-1]
        if seg.size == hi - lo + 1:
            g[lo:hi + 1] += seg
            count += 1

    if count == 0:
        return []
    g /= float(count)
    gm = float(np.max(g))
    if gm <= 0 or not np.isfinite(gm):
        return []
    g = g / gm

    out = []
    eta_list = tuple(float(x) for x in etas)
    if not eta_list:
        eta_list = (0.1, 0.2, 0.5)

    # Multiplicative step (keeps non-negativity).
    for eta in eta_list:
        b = a * np.exp(-eta * g)
        s = float(b.sum())
        if s > 1e-15:
            out.append(b * (target / s))

    # Additive clipped step is useful in low dimensions.
    if n <= 250:
        for eta in eta_list:
            b = a - eta * g
            b = np.clip(b, 0.0, None)
            s = float(b.sum())
            if s > 1e-15:
                out.append(b * (target / s))

    return out


def _random_span(rng, n, min_fraction=0.005, max_fraction=0.20):
    """Return (start, length) of a random block."""
    n = int(n)
    minw = max(1, int(n * min_fraction))
    maxw = max(minw + 1, int(n * max_fraction))
    if maxw > n:
        maxw = n
    if maxw > minw:
        w = int(rng.integers(minw, maxw + 1))
    else:
        w = max(1, minw)
    if n > w:
        st = int(rng.integers(0, n - w + 1))
    else:
        st = 0
    return st, w


def _random_perturbation(a, rng):
    """Random local modification, preserving rough scale."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a
    target = float(a.sum())
    b = a.copy()
    mode = int(rng.integers(0, 6))

    if mode == 0:
        st, w = _random_span(rng, n)
        sigma = float(rng.choice((0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.4, 0.8)))
        b[st:st + w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=w))

    elif mode == 1:
        st1, w1 = _random_span(rng, n)
        st2, w2 = _random_span(rng, n)
        frac = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.75))
        mass = float(b[st1:st1 + w1].sum())
        if mass > 0:
            b[st1:st1 + w1] *= (1.0 - frac)
            b[st2:st2 + w2] += frac * mass / max(1, w2)

    elif mode == 2:
        st, w = _random_span(rng, n)
        mean = float(b[st:st + w].mean())
        scale = max(mean, 0.1 * float(np.max(b) if b.size else 0.0), 1e-6)
        b[st:st + w] = rng.exponential(scale, size=w)

    elif mode == 3:
        b = 0.5 * (a + a[::-1])

    elif mode == 4:
        idx = int(rng.integers(0, n))
        mass = float(b[idx])
        if mass > 0:
            b[idx] = 0.0
            st2, w2 = _random_span(rng, n)
            b[st2:st2 + w2] += mass / max(1, w2)

    else:
        st, w = _random_span(rng, n)
        b[st:st + w] = 0.0

    return _clip_normalize(b, target)


def _fast_local(a, end_time, rng):
    """
    Fast local search: active-subgradient + random moves, with mild simulated
    annealing.  Returns (global_best, global_best_score).
    """
    a = _clip_normalize(a, 1.0)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a, np.inf

    current = a.copy()
    current_score = _score_array(current)

    global_best = current.copy()
    global_best_score = current_score

    if not np.isfinite(global_best_score):
        # If the initial shape is invalid (should be rare), try to repair.
        for _ in range(50):
            cand = _random_perturbation(current, rng)
            sc = _score_array(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                current = cand
                current_score = sc
                global_best = cand.copy()
                global_best_score = sc
                break
        if not np.isfinite(global_best_score):
            return global_best, global_best_score

    max_iters = 30000 if n < 40 else 6000 if n < 200 else 1500
    it = 0
    stall = 0
    temp = 0.03

    while time.monotonic() < end_time and it < max_iters:
        it += 1
        accepted_current = False

        etas = (0.08, 0.15, 0.3, 0.6) if n < 400 else (0.15, 0.35, 0.7)
        k_active = 16 if n < 150 else 8 if n < 800 else 5

        candidates = _subgradient_candidates(current, k_active, etas)
        candidates.append(_random_perturbation(current, rng))
        if n < 120:
            candidates.append(_random_perturbation(current, rng))

        for cand in candidates:
            cand = np.asarray(cand, dtype=np.float64)
            sc = _score_array(cand)
            if not np.isfinite(sc):
                continue

            improved_global = False
            if sc < global_best_score:
                global_best = cand.copy()
                global_best_score = float(sc)
                improved_global = True

            want = sc < current_score
            if not want and temp > 1e-12:
                r = float(rng.random())
                if r <= 0.0:
                    r = 1e-15
                want = math.log(r) < (current_score - sc) / temp

            if want or improved_global:
                if improved_global:
                    current = global_best.copy()
                    current_score = global_best_score
                elif want:
                    current = cand.copy()
                    current_score = float(sc)
                accepted_current = True
                break

        if not accepted_current:
            # Extra exploratory moves if the first tried set didn't help.
            if stall < 10:
                for _ in range(5):
                    cand = _random_perturbation(current, rng)
                    sc = _score_array(cand)
                    if not np.isfinite(sc):
                        continue
                    if sc < global_best_score:
                        global_best = cand.copy()
                        global_best_score = float(sc)
                        current = global_best.copy()
                        current_score = global_best_score
                        accepted_current = True
                        break
                    if sc < current_score:
                        current = cand.copy()
                        current_score = float(sc)
                        accepted_current = True
                        break
            else:
                # Reset trajectory to the best known basin and cool a little.
                current = global_best.copy()
                current_score = global_best_score
                temp = max(0.005, temp * 0.85)
                stall = 0

        if accepted_current:
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall % 25 == 0:
                temp = max(0.001, temp * 0.8)
            if stall > 400:
                break

        # If temporary path got much worse than global best, snap back.
        if (not np.isfinite(current_score)) or (current_score > global_best_score * 1.06):
            current = global_best.copy()
            current_score = global_best_score

    return global_best, global_best_score


def _soa_lp(a, b_ub, rows, n, ub, time_limit):
    """
    LP: maximize sum(g) s.t. selected convolution constraints a*g <= b_ub,
    0 <= g <= ub.

    a is the current sequence (any scale), rows are selected lags.
    """
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return None
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(n)
    rows = np.asarray(rows, dtype=int)
    rows = rows[(rows >= 0) & (rows <= 2 * n - 2)]
    if rows.size == 0 or b_ub <= 0 or not np.isfinite(b_ub):
        return None

    row_parts = []
    col_parts = []
    val_parts = []

    for k in rows:
        k = int(k)
        lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        if hi < lo:
            continue
        length = hi - lo + 1
        js = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=int)
        # For variable g_j, coefficient in coefficient k is a_{k-j}.
        vals = a[k - hi:k - lo + 1][::-1]
        vals = vals[np.isfinite(vals) & (vals > 0.0)]
        if vals.size == 0:
            continue
        if vals.size != length:
            # Keep indices consistent: vals are aligned with js.
            js_use = js
            vals = vals[:js_use.size]
            if js_use.size == 0:
                continue
        else:
            js_use = js
        row_parts.append(np.full(js_use.size, k, dtype=int))
        col_parts.append(js_use.astype(int))
        val_parts.append(vals.astype(np.float64))

    if not val_parts:
        return None

    data = np.concatenate(val_parts)
    rows2 = np.concatenate(row_parts)
    cols2 = np.concatenate(col_parts)

    A = csr_matrix((data, (rows2, cols2)), shape=(int(rows.size), n))

    c_obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, float(ub))] * n

    try:
        sol = linprog(
            c_obj,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=np.full(rows.size, float(b_ub), dtype=np.float64),
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"presolve": True, "time_limit": max(0.2, float(time_limit))},
        )
        if getattr(sol, "success", False) and getattr(sol, "x", None) is not None:
            x = np.asarray(sol.x, dtype=np.float64)
            if x.size == n and np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
                return np.clip(x, 0.0, float(ub))
    except Exception:
        pass
    return None


def _soa_optimize(a, end_time, rng):
    """
    LP-based SOTA-style updater.

    If scipy is unavailable, falls back to the generic fast local search.
    """
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return _fast_local(a, end_time, rng)

    a = _clip_normalize(a, 1.0)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n < 2:
        return a, _score_array(a)

    # Work at a moderate scale to make LP numbers healthy.
    T = math.sqrt(max(1, n)) * 2.0
    current = _clip_normalize(a, T)
    current_score = _score_array(current)

    global_best = current.copy()
    global_best_score = current_score

    steps = 0
    fails = 0
    max_steps = 160 if n <= 80 else 90 if n <= 500 else 45

    while time.monotonic() < end_time and steps < max_steps:
        steps += 1

        c = _convolution(current)
        if c.size == 0:
            break
        m = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0:
            break

        # Use all constraints for small n; top-lag constraints for large n.
        if n <= 140:
            rows = np.arange(2 * n - 1)
        else:
            top = int(min(140, max(30, 2 * n - 1)))
            if top < c.size:
                rows = np.argpartition(c, -top)[-top:]
            else:
                rows = np.arange(c.size)

        q = float(rng.choice((1.0, 1.0, 1.0, 1.2, 1.5, 2.0)))
        ub = 20.0 * T
        x = _soa_lp(current, m * q, rows, n, ub, 2.0)

        accepted = False
        if x is not None:
            s_g = float(x.sum())
            if s_g > 1e-12:
                g = x * (T / s_g)
                for mix in (0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0):
                    b = (1.0 - mix) * current + mix * g
                    b = _clip_normalize(b, T)
                    sc = _score_array(b)
                    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < global_best_score:
                        global_best = b.copy()
                        global_best_score = float(sc)
                        current = b.copy()
                        accepted = True
                        break

        if not accepted:
            fails += 1
            if fails >= 3 or steps % 8 == 0:
                cand = _random_perturbation(current, rng)
                cand = _clip_normalize(cand, T)
                sc = _score_array(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < global_best_score:
                    global_best = cand.copy()
                    global_best_score = float(sc)
                    current = cand.copy()
                    accepted = True
                    fails = 0
            if fails > 30:
                break
        else:
            fails = 0

    # A short final fast polish at unit scale.
    b, s = _fast_local(_clip_normalize(global_best, 1.0), min(end_time, time.monotonic() + 5.0), rng)
    if np.isfinite(s) and s < global_best_score:
        global_best = b
        global_best_score = s

    return _clip_normalize(global_best, 1.0), global_best_score


def _generate_inits(n, rng, best_small):
    """Generate a variety of initial shapes for a given length."""
    n = int(n)
    if n < 2:
        return []
    out = []

    if n == 2:
        out.append(np.array([2.0, 1.0], dtype=np.float64))
    if n == 4:
        out.append(np.array([1.0, 1.0, 0.0, 1.0], dtype=np.float64))

    # Uniform-ish random positives.
    for _ in range(2):
        out.append(rng.random(n) + 1e-8)

    # Binary / sparse patterns.
    for p in (0.25, 0.45, 0.70):
        out.append((rng.random(n) < p).astype(np.float64) + 1e-8)

    # Smooth centered bumps.
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    center = 0.5 * (n - 1)
    span = max(1.0, n - 1)
    for sig in (0.12, 0.25, 0.45, 0.75):
        out.append(np.exp(-((x - center) / (sig * span)) ** 2) + 1e-8)

    # Windows that vanish at endpoints.
    if n > 1:
        t = x / float(n - 1)
        for alpha in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0):
            w = 1.0 - np.clip(2.0 * np.abs(t - 0.5), 0.0, 1.0) ** alpha
            out.append(w + 1e-8)

    # Alternating high/low.
    for ratio in (0.25, 0.45, 0.65):
        alt = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        alt[1::2] = ratio
        out.append(alt + 1e-8)

    # Low-order sinusoidal perturbations of constants.
    for f in (1, 2, 3):
        phi = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        out.append(np.clip(1.0 + 0.9 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * f * x / float(n) + phi), 1e-8, None))

    # Small known patterns tiled/resized.
    for pat in ([2.0, 1.0], [1.0, 1.0, 0.0, 1.0]):
        out.append(np.resize(np.asarray(pat, dtype=np.float64), n) + 1e-8)

    # Interpolate best_small if useful.
    if best_small is not None:
        bs = np.asarray(best_small, dtype=np.float64)
        bs = bs[np.isfinite(bs)]
        m = int(bs.size)
        if m >= 2:
            if m == n:
                out.append(bs.copy())
            else:
                xs = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
                xn = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
                out.append(np.interp(xn, xs, bs) + 1e-8)

    cleaned = []
    for arr in out:
        arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
        if arr.size != n:
            continue
        arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 1e-9, 1e6)
        s = float(arr.sum())
        if s > 0:
            cleaned.append(arr * (1.0 / s))
    return cleaned


def _de_objective(u, n):
    """Objective for differential evolution.  Scale-invariant."""
    arr = np.asarray(u, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return 1e100
    a = arr / s
    v = _score_array(a)
    return float(v) if np.isfinite(v) else 1e100


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for the best sequence and return it as a Python list of floats.
    Global height_sequence_1 may be used as an additional initial shape.
    """
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total = float(budget_s) if budget_s is not None else 2400.0
    if total <= 60.0:
        total = 60.0
    deadline = t0 + total - 40.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if seed is not None else 42)

    # Safe fallbacks with official score 16/9 < 2.
    best = np.array([2.0, 1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    best_score = _score_array(best)
    print(f"t={time.monotonic() - t0:.1f}s initial best={best_score:.7f} n={best.size}", flush=True)

    # Use provided previous construction if present and final length is feasible.
    parent_arr = None
    try:
        p = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if p is not None and len(p) >= 2:
            arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
            arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
            arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if arr.size <= 8000:
                parent_arr = arr
                arr1 = _clip_normalize(arr, 1.0)
                sc = _score_array(arr1)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                    best = arr1.copy()
                    best_score = float(sc)
                    print(f"t={time.monotonic() - t0:.1f}s parent improved best={best_score:.7f} n={best.size}", flush=True)
    except Exception:
        parent_arr = None

    # Phase 1: small lengths, robust local search.
    small_lengths = [2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12, 14, 16, 20, 24, 30]
    for n in small_lengths:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now > deadline:
            break
        left = deadline - now
        if left < 1.0:
            break
        inits = _generate_inits(n, rng, best)
        if not inits:
            continue
        quick = 1.5 if n <= 5 else 3.0 if n <= 12 else 5.0
        kmax = int(max(1, min(len(inits), left // max(1.0, quick))))
        for a0 in inits[:kmax]:
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now + 1.0 > deadline:
                break
            end = min(deadline, now + quick)
            a, sc = _fast_local(a0, end, rng)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best = a.copy()
                best_score = float(sc)
                print(f"t={time.monotonic() - t0:.1f}s small n={a.size} best={best_score:.7f}", flush=True)

    # Phase 2: tiny differential evolution on low dimensions (if SciPy available).
    if HAVE_SCIPY and "differential_evolution" in globals():
        de_lengths = [3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10, 12]
        for n in de_lengths:
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now > deadline:
                break
            left = deadline - now
            if left < 12.0:
                break
            try:
                res = differential_evolution(
                    _de_objective,
                    bounds=[(1e-6, 1.0)] * n,
                    args=(n,),
                    maxiter=20,
                    popsize=10,
                    tol=0.08,
                    seed=int(rng.integers(0, 2**31 - 1)),
                    polish=False,
                    workers=1,
                )
                if getattr(res, "x", None) is not None:
                    rarr = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
                    rarr = _clip_normalize(rarr, 1.0)
                    end = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 4.0)
                    a, sc = _fast_local(rarr, end, rng)
                    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                        best = a.copy()
                        best_score = float(sc)
                        print(f"t={time.monotonic() - t0:.1f}s DE n={a.size} best={best_score:.7f}", flush=True)
            except Exception:
                pass

    # Phase 3: medium lengths with LP-based SOTA-style updates.
    medium_lengths = [40, 70, 120, 200, 320]
    if parent_arr is not None and 40 <= parent_arr.size <= 400:
        medium_lengths.append(int(parent_arr.size))
    medium_lengths = sorted(set(medium_lengths))
    for n in medium_lengths:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now > deadline:
            break
        left = deadline - now
        if left < 20.0:
            break
        budget = min(40.0, left * 0.18)
        if budget < 8.0:
            continue
        inits = _generate_inits(n, rng, best)
        if not inits:
            continue
        # Try a few very different seeds.
        kmax = int(max(1, min(3, len(inits), budget // 8.0 + 1)))
        per = budget / float(kmax)
        for a0 in inits[:kmax]:
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now + 2.0 > deadline:
                break
            end = min(deadline, now + per)
            a, sc = _soa_optimize(a0, end, rng)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best = a.copy()
                best_score = float(sc)
                print(f"t={time.monotonic() - t0:.1f}s medium n={a.size} best={best_score:.7f}", flush=True)

    # Phase 4: large lengths if time remains.  These are the most likely to beat
    # low constant-length bounds because the convolution has more lags.
    large_lengths = [500, 850, 1400, 2400, 4000]
    if parent_arr is not None and 300 <= parent_arr.size <= 6500:
        large_lengths.append(int(parent_arr.size))
    large_lengths = sorted(set(large_lengths))
    for n in large_lengths:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now > deadline:
            break
        left = deadline - now
        if left < 60.0:
            break
        budget = min(150.0, left * 0.22)
        if budget < 15.0:
            continue
        inits = _generate_inits(n, rng, best)
        # Add a perturbed parent if useful.
        if parent_arr is not None and parent_arr.size > n // 2:
            n0 = int(np.linspace(0, 1, parent_arr.size))
            xn = np.linspace(0, p_arr_size if False else 1, n)
            try:
                pi = np.interp(np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n), np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(parent_arr.size)), parent_arr)
                pi = _clip_normalize(pi, 1.0)
                inits.append(_random_perturbation(pi, rng))
                inits.append(_random_perturbation(pi, rng))
            except Exception:
                pass
        if not inits:
            continue
        kmax = int(max(1, min(3, len(inits), budget // 15.0 + 1)))
        per = budget / float(kmax)
        for a0 in inits[:kmax]:
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now + 5.0 > deadline:
                break
            end = min(deadline, now + per)
            a, sc = _soa_optimize(a0, end, rng)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best = a.copy()
                best_score = float(sc)
                print(f"t={time.monotonic() - t0:.1f}s large n={a.size} best={best_score:.7f}", flush=True)

    # Phase 5: final polish on the best sequence.
    now = time.monotonic()
    if now + 10.0 < deadline:
        if best.size <= 180:
            a, sc = _soa_optimize(best, deadline, rng)
        else:
            a, sc = _fast_local(best, deadline, rng)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best = a.copy()
            best_score = float(sc)
            print(f"t={time.monotonic() - t0:.1f}s final polish n={a.size} best={best_score:.7f}", flush=True)

    # Ensure best is a clean list.
    best = _clip_normalize(best, 1.0)
    cand = [float(x) for x in best]

    # Validate with exact local score; if something unreasonable happens, fall back.
    exact = _exact_score_list(cand)
    if not np.isfinite(exact):
        exact = np.inf

    # Also ask the official evaluator if available.
    official = np.inf
    try:
        official = float(evaluate_sequence(cand))
    except Exception:
        official = np.inf

    chosen = cand
    chosen_score = min(exact, official)

    # Verify safe fallbacks and use them if they are better.
    fallbacks = (
        [2.0, 1.0],
        [1.0, 1.0, 0.0, 1.0],
    )
    for fb in fallbacks:
        fb_score = np.inf
        try:
            fb_score = min(float(evaluate_sequence(fb)), _exact_score_list(fb))
        except Exception:
            fb_score = _exact_score_list(fb)
        if np.isfinite(fb_score) and fb_score < chosen_score:
            chosen = fb
            chosen_score = fb_score

    print(f"t={time.monotonic() - t0:.1f}s returned n={len(chosen)} score={chosen_score:.7f}", flush=True)
    return chosen
```
